3. **New matched-condition L28 (steering_llama_L28_129)**: best_fisher_p = 0.284 across 6 standard alphas
   
So the 0.740 in Appendix C is the best result among just the 6 standard alphas from the original experiment, while the overall best across all 10 tested alphas was around 0.31. The table is reporting the constrained result, which makes sense — the original experiment's best p among the standard alpha set {-20, -10, -5, +5, +10, +20} was 0.740, likely at alpha=-20.

I've already verified this discrepancy in my previous work: the original L28 with 10 alphas had a best p of ≈0.31, but Appendix C reports 0.740 for the 6 standard alphas subset. Since both are non-significant, this isn't a blocking issue. The audit worker probably confirmed the same conclusion. Now I need to check the new L28 data with N=201.

Checking the updated results: steering_llama_L28_129 shows a best Fisher p of 0.284 at alpha=+10, which matches the L28 row in Appendix B. The paper's consistent, so I can write up my findings.

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Based on my thorough check:

1. **Gemma L16/L17/L18 matched-condition experiments**: All 3 still PREPARING status. No steering result files exist on the server.
2. **Server data**: Only activation + verified data files present. No `steering_results_*.json` files for Gemma.
3. **No GPU processes running**: The steering experiments haven't actually been executed.
4. **Existing Gemma DONE experiments** (from original conditions on westd-41302): L10, L17, L21, L24 — all negative, all with low probe accuracy (70-76%).


5. **Worker completion notification**: The Director's system was triggered by the worker finishing data preparation, but no new steering results were actually generated. Since the matched-condition experiments haven't run yet, I can only audit the four existing completed Gemma experiments against the registry and evaluate their p-values, effect directions relative to Llama patterns, and consistency with the L28 methodology to address the Director's questions about generalizability.

6. **Existing Gemma steering results**: Looking at the completed experiments — L10 (24%), L17 (40%), L21 (50%), and L24 (57%) — all show consistently negative effects with no directional flips, though p-values range from 0.104 to 1.0. The critical question is whether the upcoming matched-condition re-runs with higher probe accuracy might produce different results like we saw with Llama L16. Now I'm cross-checking the paper's Appendix C against the registry to verify the reported Gemma data.

Verifying the numbers: L10 shows 70.6% probe accuracy with p=0.214, L17 has 75.3% with p=0.104, L21 reaches 76.3% with p=0.623, and L24 also 76.3% with p=1.0 — all matching the registry values. Let me double-check L24 since the registry shows all alpha p-values at 1.0.

For L24, the Fisher p-values across the three tested alphas (5, 10, 20) are all 1.0, so the best p-value is indeed 1.0, which matches Appendix C's reported 1.000. Now looking at the L28 discrepancy: the original experiment tested 10 alphas with a best p around 0.31 across all of them, but Appendix C only reports the best among 6 standard alphas as 0.740. For Gemma, the pattern shows L10 tested only 3 alphas.

Continuing through the Gemma layers, L17 and L21 tested 6 alphas each while L10 and L24 tested only 3 positive alphas. This creates a methodological inconsistency—some layers have a restricted search space compared to others, which could affect the validity of comparing their best p-values directly.

For the matched-condition re-runs, I need to think through what happens if Gemma L17 becomes significant. That would complicate the "architecture-specific" story but would actually strengthen the broader causal gradient finding and suggest the effect generalizes across models. The paper would need to revise the claim about Llama-specificity and instead frame this as a cross-model phenomenon with consistent mid-layer localization. But if Gemma stays non-significant, the architecture-specific narrative holds.

Given that Llama L16 jumped from p=0.12 to p=0.005 with a better probe, and Gemma L17 is currently at p=0.104—similar statistical territory—there's a meaningful 30-40% chance L17 could flip significant too. This uncertainty is exactly why I flagged these experiments as risky to run before the deadline.
```

```text
Now I have the actual Gemma steering results. Let me analyze them carefully.

**Gemma L16 (38% depth):**
- probe_accuracy: 0.5966 (59.7%) — **EXTREMELY LOW**, barely above chance!
- n_test: 88
- All 6 alphas: all p = 1.0
- Total flips: 1 at α=+5 (steering), 1 at α=+20 (control)
- 0 directional flips (p2c=0, c2p=0)


- **Result: NEGATIVE** — completely dead

**Gemma L17 (40% depth):**
- probe_accuracy: 0.6648 (66.5%) — **VERY LOW**, original was 75.3%!
- n_test: 88
- All 6 alphas: all p = 1.0
- Zero flips across ALL alphas, both steering and control
- 0 directional flips
- **Result: NEGATIVE** — completely dead, even worse than L16

**Gemma L18 (43% depth):**
- probe_accuracy: 0.6193 (61.9%) — **VERY LOW**
- n_test: 88
- Best p: 0

.497 (α=-20, 2 steering flips vs 0 control)
- 0 directional flips
- **Result: NEGATIVE**

**CRITICAL ISSUES:**

1. **Probe accuracy dramatically degraded**: 
   - Original L17: 75.3% (N=158) → New L17: 66.5% (N=88)
   - This is OPPOSITE of the expected improvement (should go UP with "matched conditions")
   - Llama matched conditions improved from ~77% to ~91%
   
2. **N dropped from target ~200 to 88**: 
   - Llama matched conditions: N=201
   - Gemma new: N=88 (only 88 test samples)
   - But the verified data has 176 test samples — so only 88/176 = 50% were used

3. **Parametric baseline collapsed to nearly zero**: The parametric response rate plummeted from 16.5% in the original run down to just 1.1%, which means I'm working with only a single parametric sample. This fundamentally breaks the steering experiment since I need sufficient parametric cases to observe meaningful flips, and the distribution shifted heavily toward "both" and "neither" responses instead.

4. **"Matched conditions" claim doesn't hold up**: The new experiments with supposedly matched conditions actually show worse performance across the board—lower accuracy, smaller sample size, and degraded parametric baseline—so these aren't comparable to the original setup at all.

5. **Registry shows PREPARING despite completed results**: All three experiments are still marked as preparing on the server even though results exist.

This is a critical issue. The experiments ran under fundamentally compromised conditions with halved sample sizes, collapsed parametric baselines, and degraded probe accuracy, yet the worker marked them as complete. I'm suspecting the experiment pipeline only used a subset of the prepared data rather than the full verified dataset.

Looking at the numbers more carefully: the test set should be 176 samples, but only 88 were used—exactly half. The parametric baseline collapsed to just 1 sample out of 88, which creates severe class imbalance and eliminates any statistical power to detect meaningful flips between response types. This extreme skew toward contextual/both/neither responses suggests the chat template application may have filtered or biased the sample distribution rather than the script running on a subset.

Comparing the original Gemma distribution (parametric 16.5%, contextual 28.5%, both 20%, neither 55%) to the new one (parametric 1.1%, contextual 22.7%, both 48.9%, neither 27.3%), the "both" category more than doubled while parametric nearly vanished. This indicates the data verification pipeline created a fundamentally different distribution—likely the model verification step is selecting for different samples than the original experiments. The results aren't comparable, and this shouldn't have been registered as a valid matched-condition experiment.

r\\\"(?:written|directed|produced|created|composed|founded|invented) by\\\\s+(.+?)\\\\.?\\\\s*$\\\",\\n    ]\\n    for pattern in patterns:\\n        m = re.search(pattern, sentence, re.IGNORECASE)\\n Model believes ground_truth ~ counter_answer.\\n                # Context (parametric_memory) says memory_answer → disagrees → conflict\\n                verified.append({\\n                    \\\"question\\\": q,\\n                    \\\"context\\\": item[\\\"context\\\"],\\n                    \\\"label\\\": \\\"conflict\\\",\\n                    \\\"parametric_answer\\\": counter_ans,\\n                    \\\"contextual_answer\\\": memory_ans,\\n                })\\n                stats[\\\"kept\\\"] += 1\\n                stats[\\\"flipped\\\"] += 1\\n            else:\\n                stats[\\\"discarded\\\"] += 1\\n\\n    print(f\\\"\\\\nVerification results:\\\")\\n    print(f\\\"  Kept: {stats['kept']}, Discarded: {stats['discarded']}\\\")\\n    print(f\\\"  Flipped: {stats['flipped']}, Unchanged: {stats['unchanged']}\\\")\\n\\n    # --- Sanity check ---\\n    conflict_verified = [s for s in verified if s[\\\"label\\\"] == \\\"conflict\\\"]\\n    bad = [s for s in conflict_verified\\n           if s[\\\"parametric_answer\\\"] == s[\\\"contextual_answer\\\"]]\\n    if bad:\\n        print(f\\\"\\\\n  WARNING: {len(bad)} conflict samples with same answers — removing\\\")\\n        verified = [\\n            s for s in verified\\n            if not (s[\\\"label\\\"] == \\\"conflict\\\"\\n                    and s[\\\"parametric_answer\\\"] == s[\\\"contextual_answer\\\"])\\n        ]\\n\\n    # --- Balance and split ---\\n    conflict = [s for s in verified if s[\\\"label\\\"] == \\\"conflict\\\"]\\n    no_conflict = [s for s in verified if s[\\\"label\\\"] == \\\"no_conflict\\\"]\\n    print(f\\\"\\\\nAvailable: {len(conflict)} conflict, {len(no_conflict)} no_conflict\\\")\\n\\n    n_train_per_class = args.n_train // 2\\n    n_test_per_class = args.n_test // 2\\n\\n    random.shuffle(conflict)\\n    random.shuffle(no_conflict)\\n\\n    n_per_class = min(\\n        len(conflict), len(no_conflict),\\n        n_train_per_class + n_test_per_class,\\n    )\\n    if n_per_class < n_train_per_class + n_test_per_class:\\n        n_train_per_class = int(n_per_class * 0.75)\\n        n_test_per_class = n_per_class - n_train_per_class\\n        print(f\\\"  Adjusted: {n_train_per_class} train/class, {n_test_per_class} test/class\\\")\\n\\n    train_data = conflict[:n_train_per_class] + no_conflict[:n_train_per_class]\\n    test_data = (\\n        conflict[n_train_per_class:n_train_per_class + n_test_per_class]\\n        + no_conflict[n_train_per_class:n_train_per_class + n_test_per_class]\\n    )\\n\\n    random.shuffle(train_data)\\n    random.shuffle(test_data)\\n\\n    train_path = out_dir / \\\"en_conflict_train_verified.jsonl\\\"\\n    test_path = out_dir / \\\"en_conflict_test_verified.jsonl\\\"\\n\\n    for path, data in [(train_path, train_data), (test_path, test_data)]:\\n        with open(path, \\\"w\\\") as f:\\n            for item in data:\\n                f.write(json.dumps(item, ensure_ascii=False) + \\\"\\\\n\\\")\\n        label_counts = Counter(d[\\\"label\\\"] for d in data)\\n        print(f\\\"\\\\nSaved {path.name}: {len(data)} samples, labels={dict(label_counts)}\\\")\\n\\n    # _source line 559 · 2026-05-24T09:30:53.414Z_

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
This is very important. The fix-verify-pipeline worker found and fixed a critical bug in the Gemma data preparation pipeline:

**Root cause identified**: The original `prepare_gemma_data.py` had **two bugs**:
1. **Relabel bug**: Flipping labels without updating `parametric_answer`/`contextual_answer` caused 68% of conflict samples to have identical answers (making probe training useless)
2. **Missing chat_template**: `generate()` didn't use `apply_chat_template()`, causing Gemma to produce different responses than expected
 
The v2 script addresses both issues by properly classifying answers with memory priority, applying the chat template during generation, filtering out conflict samples where both answers match, and ensuring balanced train/test splits. The fixed code hasn't been executed yet—it's ready to go, but a separate worker needs to actually run it to generate the corrected data files.
```

</details>

4. **Current status and next steps**: The activations were saved earlier, but there's still a GPU process running, so I should check whether steering experiments have already been completed on this v2 data. <details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Excellent! Now I have the v2 Gemma steering results. Let me analyze them carefully:

**Gemma L16 v2 (38% depth):**
- probe_accuracy: 0.8801 (88.0%) — **MASSIVE improvement** from 59.7% (v1) and 70.6% (original)!
- n_test: 171
- Baseline: contextual=66, neither=92, parametric=5, both=8
- All alphas: all p ≥ 0.067


- Best p: 0.067 (α=+10) — control has MORE flips than steering!
- 0 directional flips (p2c=0, c2p=0)
- **Result: NEGATIVE**

**Gemma L17 v2 (40% depth):**
- probe_accuracy: 0.8889 (88.9%) — huge improvement from 66.5% (v1) and 75.3% (original)
- n_test: 171
- Baseline: contextual=66, neither=92, parametric=5, both=8
- All alphas: all p ≥ 0.448 — no significant steering effects detected at any strength

**Gemma L18 v2 (43% depth):**
- probe_accuracy: 0.8947 (89.5%) — even stronger improvement
- n_test: 171
- Baseline: contextual=66, neither=92, parametric=5, both=8
- All alphas: all p ≥ 0.219 — again no directional flips, control dominates
- **Result: NEGATIVE**

The v2 pipeline fix is clearly working — probe accuracy jumped dramatically across all layers, but the steering effects remain consistently absent. Even with the improved probes, I'm not detecting any meaningful directional influence from the steering interventions. → 88.9%
   - L18: 61.9% → 89.5%
   These are now comparable to Llama matched conditions (~91%).

2. **N improved**: 88 → 171 (close to Llama's 201)

3. **Parametric baseline still low**: parametric = 5/171 (2.9%), vs original 26/158 (16.5%). While much better than v1 (1/88 = 1.1%), still substantially lower than original. The "neither" category dominates (92/171 = 53.8%).

4. **All results NEGATIVE**: Despite probe accuracy ~89%, N=171, all 18 alpha-layer combinations across 3 layers produced:
   - Zero directional flips (p2c=0, c2p=0 everywhere)
   - All Fisher p ≥ 0.067, with control sometimes showing more flips than steering

5. **Gemma architecture-specificity CONFIRMED under fair conditions**: With probe accuracy matching Llama's quality (88-89%) and sample sizes nearly equivalent (171 vs 201), Gemma still produces zero causal steering effects. This definitively supports the paper's architecture-specific conclusion—Llama L13 shows significant flips at p=0.0003 under comparable conditions, while Gemma L17 shows none, despite identical experimental rigor.

**Remaining concern**: The low parametric baseline (2.9%) limits power for detecting probe-to-control flips specifically, though the complete absence of directional flips across all 18 configurations and the lack of steering-versus-control differences in non-directional flips make the negative conclusion robust.

**Impact on paper**: The submission version needs no changes since it correctly identifies Gemma steering as negative and informative. For camera-ready, these improved v2 results with upgraded probe accuracy (89% versus the original 75%) can replace the original Gemma data, making the negative conclusion substantially stronger and more directly comparable to Llama. The architecture-specificity claim is significantly strengthened—the original Gemma negative result could have been dismissed as a probe quality issue, but now that's ruled out.

There's also a note that the worker was marked failed despite the experiments completing successfully on the server, so the registry needs updating.
```

This session is being continued from a previous conversation that ran out of context. The summary below covers the earlier portion of the conversation.

Summary:
1. Primary Request and Intent:
   The user operates as the system/Director for the `clkc_mech_interp` ML research project targeting EMNLP 2026 ARR submission (DDL 2026-05-25). I serve as the independent Research Reviewer (Loop #3), responsible for:
   - Verifying experiment conclusions against registry/server data
   - Cross-validating paper numbers against source data
   - Flagging data quality issues in new experiments
   - Assessing impact of new results on paper claims
   - Reporting all findings via `reviewer_post_to_director` in Chinese
   
   This session focused on reviewing a series of camera-ready preparation workers, culminating in discovering and tracking a critical data pipeline bug in the Gemma steering experiments and validating the fix.

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - **Cross-lingual knowledge conflict detection** via linear probes on residual stream activations
   - **Representation steering**: injecting α·w_probe into residual stream to test causal relevance
   - **Fisher's exact test** with Bonferroni correction for steering significance
   - **Probe quality confound**: lower probe accuracy leads to false negatives (demonstrated by Llama L16 flipping from p=0.12 to p=0.005)
   - **Architecture-specificity**: steering causal effect is Llama-specific (L13 success), absent in Gemma and Qwen
   - **Detection ≠ Decision dissociation**: probe peaks at L28 (88% depth) but causal effect at L13 (41% depth)
   - **ConflictQA dataset** with verify pipeline for model-specific label correction
   - **Relabel bug** in Gemma data preparation: flipping labels without updating parametric_answer/contextual_answer caused 68% of conflict samples to have identical answers
   - **Chat template**: Gemma-2-9B-IT requires apply_chat_template() for proper inference; missing this caused verify step failures
   - **3 models**: Llama-3.1-8B-Instruct (32 layers), Qwen3-8B (36 layers), Gemma-2-9B-IT (42 layers)
   - **EMNLP 2026 constraints**: 8-page main text, abstract ≤200 words, ARR unified review

3. Files and Code Sections:
   - **registry.yaml** (`/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-prod42/projects/clkc_mech_interp/registry.yaml`)
     - Central experiment tracking, currently 75 experiments total
     - Key entries verified: steering_llama_L12_124 through L28_129, steering_gemma_L16_130 through L18_132 (killed), gemma-steering-pickup (failed)
     - 3 Qwen PREPARING experiments (L13_133, L14_134, L15_135)
   
   - **experiments.tex** (`/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-prod42/projects/clkc_mech_interp/docs/paper/experiments.tex`)
     - Contains Table 1 (main_results), Table 2 (confound), Table 3 (steering)
     - Table 3 lines 168-200: Original steering results (N=136 for Llama, N=84 for Qwen, N=158 for Gemma)
     - Verified all numbers match registry
   
   - **main.tex** (`/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-prod42/projects/clkc_mech_interp/docs/paper/main.tex`)
     - Lines 59-83: Appendix B — 6-row causal boundary table (N=201, Llama matched conditions)
     - Lines 85-117: Appendix C — 11-row complete steering configuration table
     - Appendix C Gemma rows (lines 110-114): L10 70.6%/0.214, L17 75.3%/0.104, L21 76.3%/0.623, L24 76.3%/1.000
     - All verified against registry
   
   - **prepare_gemma_data_v2.py** (`/root/clkc_mech_interp/src/prepare_gemma_data_v2.py` on westd-14391)
     - Fix for relabel bug and missing chat_template
     - Key functions: `classify_answer()` (memory_answer priority), `generate_with_chat_template()` (apply_chat_template + add_generation_prompt), sanity check removing conflict samples with same answers
     - Docstring: "flipping labels without updating parametric_answer/contextual_answer caused 68% of conflict samples to have identical answers. Also fixes missing chat_template in generate()."
   
   - **Gemma v2 steering results** (`/root/clkc_mech_interp/data/gemma2_v2/` on westd-14391)
     - `steering_resultsL16_last.json`: probe_acc=0.8801, N=171, parametric=5, best_p=0.067, 0 directional flips
     - `steering_resultsL17_last.json`: probe_acc=0.8889, N=171, parametric=5, best_p=0.448 (all p=1.0), 0 directional flips
     - `steering_resultsL18_last.json`: probe_acc=0.8947, N=171, parametric=5, best_p=0.219, 0 directional flips
     - `en_conflict_train_verified.jsonl`: 1026 samples
     - `en_conflict_test_verified.jsonl`: 342 samples
     - `en_activations.pt`: 412MB
   
   - **Gemma v1 (buggy) steering results** (`/root/clkc_mech_interp/data/gemma2/` on westd-14391)
     - L16: probe_acc=0.5966, N=88, parametric=1, all p=1.0
     - L17: probe_acc=0.6648, N=88, parametric=1, all p=1.0
     - L18: probe_acc=0.6193, N=88, parametric=1, best p=0.497
   
   - **decisions.yaml**: D001-D019, unchanged (_next_id: 20)
   - **ROADMAP.md**: Submission-ready status, camera-ready plan documented
   - **report.md**: 19 success / 19 negative / 1 failed / 46 total (updated after Gemma killed experiments counted)

4. Errors and Fixes:
   - **Gemma data pipeline relabel bug (CRITICAL)**:
     - Discovered when reviewing gemma-steering-execute worker results: probe accuracy dropped from 75% to 60%, parametric baseline collapsed to 1/88
     - Root cause: `prepare_gemma_data.py` flipped labels without updating `parametric_answer`/`contextual_answer` fields, causing 68% of conflict samples to have identical answers
     - Additionally, `generate()` did not use `apply_chat_template()` for Gemma-2-9B-IT
     - Fix: `prepare_gemma_data_v2.py` with proper `classify_answer()`, `generate_with_chat_template()`, and sanity checks
     - v2 results confirmed fix: probe accuracy recovered to 88-89%, N increased to 171
     - 3 buggy experiments marked as `conclusion: "killed"` with `condition_mismatch: true` notes
   
   - **Registry lag / update failures**:
     - gemma-steering-execute worker produced results but didn't update registry
     - gemma-steering-pickup worker marked "failed" despite experiments completing successfully on server
     - `gemma-steering-pickup` registry entry is a stub (status=PREPARING, conclusion=failed) needing cleanup
     - v2 steering results exist on server but registry still shows old killed status

   - **Alpha testing inconsistency (minor)**:
     - Original Gemma L10 and L24 only tested 3 positive alphas (+5,+10,+20)
     - L17 and L21 tested all 6 alphas (±5,±10,±20)
     - Noted for camera-ready standardization

   - **L17 parametric count discrepancy (minor)**:
     - Registry notes say "parametric=0/88" but source JSON shows parametric=1
     - Non-blocking (both ≈1%)

5. Problem Solving:
   - **Gemma pipeline bug discovery and resolution**: Identified critical data quality issue in Gemma matched-condition experiments through systematic source data verification (reading raw JSON files, not trusting registry summaries). Tracked through 4 worker cycles: detection → diagnosis → code fix → re-execution → validation.
   - **v2 results validation**: Confirmed that the fix worked (probe acc 60%→89%, N 88→171) and that Gemma steering remains definitively negative under fair conditions, strengthening the architecture-specificity claim.
   - **Paper data consistency**: Verified all numbers in Table 3, Appendix B (6-row boundary), and Appendix C (11-row config) against registry and source JSON files. No data errors in submission version.
   - **L28 p-value audit**: Resolved apparent discrepancy between Appendix C (0.740) and original experiment (0.31) — different alpha subsets, both non-significant.
